EXHIBIT F Lighting Plan 
LIGHTING PLAN 
FOR THE UTAH OLYMPIC PARK SPECIALLY PLANNED AREA 
3419 OLYMPIC PARKWAY,PARKCITY, SUMMIT COUNTY, UTAH 
December 5, 2012 
The below statements correlate with the Utah Olympic Park Resort Center SPA Submittal, Exhibit B: 
Final Site Plans on sheet 19 showing conceptual locations. 
Lighting Standards within Utah Olympic Park shall conform to the Snyderville Basin Development Code 
Section 10-4-22 unless otherwise identified to deviate from those standards below. These standards are 
intended to allow reasonable enjoyment and practical use of all Olympic Park venues, facilities and 
amenities, providing adequate lighting levels of parking and pedestrian areas, while also protecting 
adjacent property owners and residents of Snyderville Basin. 
GENERAL STANDARDS 
High-pressure sodium is the lamp source to be utilized throughout the Snyderville Basin for all roadway, 
pathway, area and building facade illumination. Sport facility or outdoor recreation lighting is the only 
application where a metal halide lamp source may by used, subject to the limitati0ns herein. No 
floodlighting shall be pennitted. Light Standards may include prefabricatt;d or cust0m poles, arms and 
fixtures to complement the existing and proposed architectural and signage styles. Fixtures shall be of a 
full cutoff variety. Foot Candles shall meet or exceed the requirements within the €ode for each 
respective Light type. 
Lighting use will generally plan and implement fixture use so as t0 miµimize bleeding/spillover. 
Additionally, as technology and light fixture efficiencies are improved over time, the Utah Olympic Park 
commits to replace outdated fixtures with new energy efficient light.fixtures as.finances allow. 
LIGHTING APPLICATIONS 
Parking Lot Lighting 
A. For new parking areas, parking lot fixtures shall not be mo11ntedm0Fe than si~teen feet (16') 
above grade, as measured to the top of the fixture or a h0ri20ntal plane being lit by th.e fixture. 
Existing parking lot fixtures with above grade heights of twenty-four feet (24') are aUowed. Any 
enlargements to existing parking lots shall allow fixtures to 1:11atch the height, style, and type of 
existing fixtures. 
B. The fixture lamp shall not exceed four hundred ( 400) watts per p0le. 
C. Fixtures shall be used in interior parking/site installations only. 
D. Timers and motion sensor devices shall be used wherever practical to minimize light pollution 
within the Snyderville Basin. 
Roadway Lighting 
A. Roadway pole fixtures shall not exceed twenty-five feet (25') in height. 
B. Fixtures should house a high-pressure sodium lamp, not to exceed one hundred fifty (150) 
watts/pole. 
C. Fixtures should have die-cast aluminum housing, and shal'l be a type Ii, III or IV distribution 
pattern. 
D. Decorative roadway pole-mounted fixtures: 
• Shall not be mounted above sixteen feet (16') above grade. 
• Shall house a high-pressure sodium lamp, with no more than one hundred fifty (150) 
watts/pole. 
OCTOBER 2013 UTAH OLYMPIC PARK SPA PLAN UPDATED BOOK OF EXHIBITS I 44 
Exhibit 3: Redlined Proposed DA 4th Amended
71
EXHIBIT F Lighting Plan (continued) 
Area Lighting, Pedestrian Walkways, Plazas, etc. 
A. New area lighting fixtures shall not be mounted more than twelve feet (12') above grade directly 
below the fixture, as measured to the top of the fixture or a horizontal plane being lit by the 
fixture. For existing area lighting, any enlargements or modifications to these areas would look 
. to match those heights and lighting fixture type. 
B. The fixture shall house a high-pressure sodium lamp, not to exceed one hundred fifty (150) watts 
per pole. 
Building Canopy/Soffit Lighting 
A. !flighting an area with fixtures mounted on a canopy, or off of a soffit of a building, the fixture 
cannot be mounted above twenty feet (20'), as measured from the top of the fixture to the adjacent 
grade or the horizontal plane being lit by the fixture. Such lighting shall be minimized to the 
extent possible. 
B. The fixture should house a high-pressure sodium lamp, not to exceed one hundred fifty (150) 
watts. 
C. Canopy/soffit-mounted fixtures should be mounted a minimum distance of seventy feet (70') 
from the circulation area, or other critical light cutoff boundaries. 
D. Lights should be integrated into the buildings architectural features in order to maximize 
effectiveness while minimizing exposure. 
Building Facade Lighting 
Any proposal for building facade lighting is subject to County approval. As a general rule it will not be 
allowed. It may be considered if the following apply: 
A. The building surface being lit is not in the line of sight of any residential living unit. 
B. The facade lighting shall be turned off by ten o'clock (10:00 p.m.) each night, and not turned on 
until dusk the following day. 
Sport Lighting 
A. Sport lighting fixtures should not be mounted above seventy feet (70'), as measured from the top 
of the fixture to the adjacent grade or the horizontal plane being lit by the fixture. 
B. Fixtures should house lamps not to exceed one thousand five hundred (1,500) watts. 
C. Lighting for outdoor recreation uses should be shut off no later than eleven o'clock (11 :00 
p.m.). Lighting dm:ation may need to be expanded periodically for special events, facility 
maintenance that includes such activities as: ice making on the bobsled track, and snowmaking on 
the ski jumps. 
Landscape/Holiday Lighting 
Lighting may occur year round within public plaza ot public use spaces for holiday, seasonal or landscape 
lighting to include strand lighting on trees, over pedestrian spaces or up lighting on landscape or 
sculptural elements within or adjacent to these spaces. 
Existing Facility Lighting 
A. It is recognized that the original Utah Winter Sports Park (now named Utah Olympic Park) was 
built under the State of Utah oversight in the early 1990s. Therefore, lighting standards and 
facility specifications do not completely conform to local code. 
B. Where financially practical and prudent, the Utah Athletic Foundation commits to update and 
replace old and failing fixtures to meet local code.

Exhibit 3: Redlined Proposed DA 4th Amended

EXHIBIT F Lighting Plan (continued)

EXHIBIT G Comprehensive Sign Plan 
COMPREHENSIVE SIGN PLAN FOR THE 
UTAH OLYMPIC PARK SPECIALLY PLANNED AREA 
3419 OLYMPIC PARKWAY, PARK CITY, SUMMIT COUNTY, UTAH 
December 5, 2012 
The below statements correlate with the Utah Olympic Park Resort Center SPA Submittal, Exhibit B: 
Final Site Plans on sheet 19 showing conceptual locations. 
A Comprehensive Sign Plan for Utah Olympic Park is permitted under the Snyderville Basin 
Development Code Section 10-8-2: Sign Regulations. Within the previously approved Utah Olympic 
Park Comprehensive Sign Plan dated October 24, 2007, Utah Olympic Park and Summit County agreed 
to memorialize certain pre-existing 2002 OlympjcSignage. Certain approvals shall continue for existing 
Utah Olympic Park signage within the current Development Agreement. 
GENERAL 
Within Utah Olympic Park, businesses and activity areas should be identified by an appropriate blend of 
permitted sign types including an Olympic Park Monument Sign, business area entry signs, freestanding 
signs, far;:ade mounted signs, projecting signs, suspended or supported signs, banners, directional signs, 
and window signs. Materials for all signs shall be compatible with the. sur,rounding architectures and 
mountain environment. Materials and color schemes of all exposed surfaces of signs shall complement 
the building located on the premises. • • 
Signs will generally conform to the character and sizes represented in the: attached illustrat,iohs. Signs 
shall be placed so as not to detract from the architectural elements an~ shall be located so as not to cause 
interruption to other architectural elements of buildings. Wood, metals, and materials simi'lar in 
appearance to existing facility architecture, signage, and the surrounding natural environment are the • 
materials of preference. • • 
HISTORICAL 2002 OLYMPIC WINTER GAMES SIGNAGE 
Olympic Towers 
Two Olympic towers exist, one at the roundabout entry to Olympic Parkway and one in front of the 
Olympic Plaza. They serve as permanent reminders of Utah's 2002 Olympic Winter Games and 
ongoing Olympic Legacy. Periodic maintenance on these towers includes rewrapping oftfie Olympic 
winter sport graphics and painting of the metal structures. Upkeep and maintenance of these towers is 
the sole responsibility of the Utah Athletic Foundation under license agreement with the United States 
Olympic Committee. The towers may be lit so long as they comply with County down lighting 
regulations. 
Existing Scoreboards 
The electronic scoreboards installed for the 2002 Olympics (20'x 60' in size) are permitted as shown 
in the attached visual. Any change in size or location is subject to Low Impact Permit review. 
Existing Flags 
The national flags located at the top and base of the jumping hills and sunounding the sliding track 
are permitted. • 
"Look of the Games"

Exhibit 3: Redlined Proposed DA 4th Amended

EXHIBIT G Comprehensive Sign Plan (continued) 
Numerous buildings and sport support areas have graphics and Olympic marks that are historical and 
appropriate in their continued visibility. Examples include logo etchings on facility windows, large 
banners with winter athlete pictures, and numerous renditions of Olympic rings displays. These are 
permitted for continued visual display. 
UTAH OLYMPIC PARK MONUMENT ENTRY SIGN 
One project entry monument sign for the development is aHowed to represent Utah Olympic Park on a 
permanent basis. The entry sign may be designed to accommodate non-electronic changeable copy 
displays and/or banners affixed to the structure or other similar means promoting civic or special events. 
The size of the individual letters of the civic or special event shall be proportional to the size of the 
structure and will be as approved through a Low Impact Permit. 
Sign Location and Size 
The sign shall be located similar to previously approved in the October 24, 2007 Utah Olympic Park 
Comprehensive Sign Plan at the southwest corner of SR-224 and Olympic Parkway in the Summit 
County right~of-way. All parts of the sign and any landscaping above grade shall be located at least 
ten feet from the back of the curb. The display area of the sign shall not exceed forty-five (45) square 
feet. 
Sign Type and Material 
The monument sign shall be constructed with a monument base of stone, wood, concrete or a 
combination of the three. The display area (sign board) shall be made ofreal metal (non-reflective), 
wood, wood composite material, stone, or similar. Sign copy may be applied, painted, engraved, 
wood, or solid letters applied directly on the signboard. Colors may include Utah Olympic Park 
colors or colors which are neutral and compatible with the surrounding architectufes and mountain 
environment. 
Sign Copy 
The sign will include the permanent copy of "Utah Olympic Park" and may include the Utah Olympic 
Park logo. The copy "USA", the Olympic Rings, and the copy "U.S. Olympic Training Site" may be 
also included iri the f~cility identification. Cha_ngeable copy is intended to promote civic or special 
events. 
BUSINESS AREA ENTRY SIGNS 
Multiple business area entry signs for the development are allowed to represent business partner locations 
on a permanent basis. The project entry sign may include individual tenant names and loc~tions. The 
si2:e of the individual letters of the proje,ct name and individual t~nants shall be propqrtioria1· t? the size of 
the strncture. . • • • • • 
Sign Location and Size . . 
Signs shall be located at the entry location of the various building clusters as conceptually identified 
in sheet 19 of the Resort Center SP A Submittal Site Plans. The display area of the sign shall not 
exceed thirty (30) square feet. 
Sign Type and Material . . 
Signs shall be constructeq with a base of stone, wood, concrete or a combination of the three. • The 
display area (sign board)" shall be made of metal (non-reflective), wood, .wood composite materi~l, or 
Coroplast. The.copy may be applied, painted, engraved, wood, o~ solid letters applied directly on the 
signboard. Colofs may include the Utah Olympic Park colors or colors which are neutral and 
compatible with the surrounding architectures and mountain environment.

EXHIBIT G Comprehensive Sign Plan ( continued) 
Sign Copy 
The sign may include company or brand names. Discounts on activities shall not be advertised. 
FREESTANDING SIGNS 
Existing signs, as shown in attached illustrations, are permitted. New signs or ch~nges to existiqg signs 
are subject to Low Impact Permit review. Signs may be wood, metal, or a material that complements the 
building. The copy may be applied, painted, eBgraved, wood, or solid letters applied directly on the 
signboard. Colors rnay include the Utah,Olympic Park color,s or colors which ate neutral and ·compatible 
with the smrounding architectures and mountain enviromnent ·Sign copy shall identify a building or area, 
be directional in nature, or inter,pretive relative to the natural enviromnent, Utah's 2002 Olympic W•inter 
Games, or the ongoing Olympic Legacy. The copy shall not include advertisements, brand names or 
logos, excluding the Utah Olympic Park n.ame and logo. 
FACADE MOUNTEB ·SIGNS 
E_xisting signs, a~ shown fr:1attached illustrations, are permitted. New signs· or changes to existing signs 
are subject to Low Impact Permit review. Signs may be wood, metal, or a materialthat complements the 
buildin.g. The copy may be applied, painted, engraved, wood, or solid letters applied directly on the 
signboard. Colors may include the Utah Olympic Park colors or colors which are neutral and compatible 
with the surrounding architectures and mountain environment. Sign copy shall identify, the ~uildi~g or be 
directional in nature. The copy shall not include advertisements, brand names or logos, exc~U:ding the 
Utah Olympic Park name and logo. 
PROJECTING SIGNS 
The bottom of projecting signs shall be a sufficient height above ground to prevent damage or hazardous 
conditions. Signs shall be proportionate in scale to their respective buildings and are subject to Low 
Impact Permit review and approval. 
SUSPENDED OR SUPPORTED SIGNS 
The bottom of suspended or supported signs shall be located sufficiently and at a height sufficient to 
prevent damage or hazardous conditions. Signs shall be proportionate in scale to their respective 
buildings and are subject to Low Impact Permit review and approval. 
BANNERS & FLAGS 
Parking Lot Banners 
B~ners may be instaHed within parking lots. The bottom of the banners shall be a sufficient 'height 
above the parki0g lot or sidewalk to prevent damage or hazardous conditions. The size of the banner 
shall be proportional with the light pole on which it is placed. These banners will be made of nylon 
or other strong weather resistant fabric material. Each banner may contain artistic expressions and 
colors. Banners may display the Utah Olympic Park name and logo and/or advertise certain events. 
Pedestrian Area Banners 
Banners may be installed on decorative and/or light poles and building facades throughout Utah 
Olympic Park. The purpose of these banBers is to animate and identify key pedestrian environments. 
The bottom of the banners shall be a sufficient height above ground to prevent damage or hazardous 
conditions. The size of the banner shall be proportional with the pole oh which it is placed. Each 
banner may contain artistic expressions and colors. Banners may contain the Utah Olympic Park 
name and logo, the name and/or logo of certain tenants within Utah Olympic Park, and niay advertise 
certain events.

Exhibit 3: Redlined Proposed DA 4th Amended

EXHIBIT G Comprehensive Sign Plan (continued) 
DIRECTIONAL SIGNS 
Pedestrian & Vehicular Directional Signs 
Additional pedestrian and vehicular directional signs may be installed with the purpose of increasing 
safety, easing vehicular traffic, identifying pedestrian traffic areas, and differentiating between 
vehicular and pedestrian areas. 
Existing Directional Signs 
The directional sign on Olympic Parkway was installed by the Utah Department of Transportation 
prior to the 2002 Olympics and is permitted. Other directional signs, examples of which can be seen 
in Exhibit D, are necessary to direct patrons around the site and are permitted. 
WINDOW SIGNS 
Signs may be stenciled or etched on windows or doors and may contain the Utah Olympic Park name and 
logo, the name and/or logo of certain tenants within Utah Olympic Park, or hours of operation. 
SIGN ILLUMINATION 
Sign lighting shall comply with lighting standards established in the Snyderville Basin Development 
Code. Lighting for all signs, if illuminated and with the exception of the scoreboard, shall be from 
external illumination sources. However, back lighting is permitted so long as the light source is screened 
from view. All external illumination sources shall be shielded from public view. Light shall be 
concentrated on the sign face. The amount of light cast to areas other than the sign shall be reduced to the 
extent possible. 
Exposed neon tubing and/or individual light bulbs forming the sign copy shall not be permitted without 
approval of O.A. Exposed neon signs shall be permitted inside a business.if set bock ten ( 10) feet 
minimum from the storefront window (with permission). 
PROHIBITED SIGNS 
Unless otherwise addressed in this Comprehensive Sign Plan, those signs identified in Section 10-8-2(F) 
of the Snyderville Basin Development Code are prohibited unless approved by separate permit. 
PERMITS REQUIRED 
Except as provided herein, no sign shall be erected without securing any permits required under 
Snyderville Basin Development Code Section 10-8-2: Sign Regulations and ariy other permits specifically 
required hereunder. 
MINIMUM STANDARD 
These guidelines constitute a minimum standard for signs. The developer intends to operate above the 
minimum standard in terms of quality and expression.

EXCHANGE AGREEMENT 
This Exchange Agreement ("Agreement") is made as of this __ day of _____ , 2013 
(the "Effective Date") by and between Summit County, a political subdivision of the State of 
Utah (the "County"), Snyderville Basin Special Recreation District, a local district of the 
State of Utah (the "District") and Utah Athletic Foundation, a Utah non-profit corporation 
(the "UAF") (individually referred to herein as a "Party" and jointly referred to herein as the 
"Parties"). The County and the District are collectively referred to herein as the "County 
Parties". 
WITNESSETH 
The County Parties are the fee title owners of a certain parcel of. real property identified 
as all of Lot 3 of the Kimball Junction Sllbdivision, First Amended, according to the official plat 
thereof recorded on November 4, 2010, as Entry Number 009097 55 in the Office of the Summit 
County Recorder, Book 2054 beginning at Page 0429 (the "!(JS Lot 3"). KJS L0t 3 is currently 
encumbered by that certain Declaration and Notice of Use Restri~ti0ns, dated November 20, 
2008, and recorded as Entry Number 00910221 in the 0ffice of the County Reco~der, Summit 
County, Utah, in Book 2055 beginning at Page 0792 (the "Covenant"); 
UAF is the fee tide owner of a certain parcel of r,eal proper,ty ide11tified' as all of Lot 5 of 
the Kimball Junction Subdivision, First Amended(~~ "l(JS Lot fn; • • • • 
. . . . 
UAF is seeking approval of a Development Agreement with .thy County to develop 
approximately seventy-one (71) acres within the Utah Winter Sports Park. ~JS Lot 5 is located 
within a visual corridor which the County desires to protect from development. In order to 
minimize the visibility of the UAF development, UAF and ·the Cou11ty have agr,eed to effectuate 
an exchange of properties in order to relocate development outside of the visuafcorridor; 
To accomplish the purposes of an exchange, U AF is wi:lling to deed to the County 
Parties a portion of KJS Lot 5, as more fully set forth in Exhibit A hereto (the "KJS Open 
Space"), together with 11.31 acres of land within the Utah Winter Sports Park that is adjacenfto 
KJS Lot 2, as more fully set forth in Exhibit B hereto (the "UOP Open Space") (together, the 
"County Open Space"), and the County Parties are willing to deed to UAF 5.22 acres ofland in 
KJS Lots 3and 5 which are adjacent to the Utah Winter Sports Park, as more fully set forth in 
Exhibit C h.ereto (the "UAF: Exparisi'on Parcel"). Prior to the qdsing, .the Cciunty Parties will 
secure a release or" the Covenant and any other development or use restrictions on the UAF 
Expansion Parcel; 
The County has determined that the UAF Expansion Parcel is Real Property Not in 
Public Use under Summit County Code § 1-11-4. The Parties have commissioned an 
independent appraisal of the various properties and have found that the fair market value of the

63% .interest in the County Open Space.

7. Entire Agreement; Amendment Waiver. This Agreement constitutes the entire 
agreem.enf b>etween the parties pertaining to any right or interest in and to the County 
Open Space and UAF Expansion PaFcel and supersedes all ,prior agreements, 
representations, and understandings of the parties. No supplement, modification, or 
amendment of .this Agreement shall be binding unless executed in writing b>y all parties. 
No waiver of an.y of the provisions of this Agreement shaU be deemed, or shaH constitute, 
a waiver of any other provisions, whether or not similar, nor shall any waiver constitute a 
continuing :waiver. No waiver shaH be ·binding unless executed in wr,iting by the party 
making the waiver. Oril,y a ;wfiting executed by all parties clearly stating its termination 
may cancel this Agreement. 
8. Severability. Any provision hereof which is prohibited or unenforceable in any 
jurisdiction will, as to such jurisdiction, be ineffective to the extent of such prohibition or 
unenforceability without invalidating the remaining provisions hereof, and any such 
prohibition or unenforceability in any jurisdiction will not invalidate or render 
unenforceable such provision in any other jurisdiction. 
9. Authority. Each Party represents and warrants that it has full authority to perfonn as 
herein provided, and has received all consents necessary or desirable in connection with 
the execution of this Agreement. 
10. Execution. This Agreement may be executed by the execution of one of more 
counterparts and may b>e executed by sending an electronic copy of an executed 
Agreement to the other party hereto. 
11. Dispute Resolution. The parties agree that any dispute, arising related to this Agreement 
may first be sub>mitted to mediation. If mediation is mutually agreed upon then, the 
dispute shall be submitted to mediation through a mediation provider unanimously agreed 
upon by the parties. Each party agrees to bear its own costs of mediation. If, however, 
any party is required to take legal action to pursue any rights under this Agreement, then 
the prevailing party shall be entitled to attorneys' fees and costs. Each of the Parties 
agrees that the other Parties shall be entitled to enforce specifically this Agreement and 
the terms and provisions hereof in any action instituted in any court having jurisdiction 
over the Parties and the matter, in addition to any other remedy to which they may be 
entitled, at law or in equity. 
12. No Brokers. All parties represent to each other that they have not engaged a real estate 
broker or other professional to whom monies· might be owed by reason of this

KIMBALL JUNCTION, SUMMIT COUNTY, UTAH 3 
THIS 4TH AMENDED DEVELOPMENT AGREEMENT is entered into as of this 4 
____day of ______, 2025, between the Utah Athletic Foundation, dba Utah Olympic 5 
Legacy Foundation (“UOLF”) , a Utah non-profit corporation ("Developer") and Summit6 
County, a political subdivision of the State of Utah, by and through its County Council 7 
(the "County"), for the Utah Olympic Park Specially Planned Area ("Olympic Park SPA"). 8 
The County and Developer are individually referred to herein as a "Party" and jointly 9 
referred to herein as the "Parties." 10 
Article 1 11 
DEFINITIONS 12 
*Any term not defined herein shall default to the definition within the Snyderville13 
Basin Development Code.14 
1.1 Affordable Athlete and Workforce Housing Unit (“AAWHU”) means an 15 
AWHU that is dedicated to fulfilling affordable housing requirements as 16 
delineated in Exhibit H. 17 
18 
1.2 Approved Uses means the approved uses which are shown on the Land Use 19 
and Zoning Charts, which are included in the Olympic Park SPA Plan Book of 20 
Exhibits as Exhibit C and may or may not be shown on the Conceptual Master 21 
Plan included as Exhibit B. 22 
23 
1.3 Architectural Design Guidelines means the Architectural Design Guidelines for 24 
the Project, a copy of which is included in the Olympic Park SPA Plan Book of 25 
Exhibits as Exhibit D. 26 
27 
1.4 Athlete and Workforce Housing Unit (“AWHU”) means a housing 28 
unit as designated by Utah Olympic Legacy Foundation for the use of 29 
Athletes and Workforce training or working at the Utah Olympic Park. 30 
AWHUs can leased on a long-term or short-term basis with priority 31 
given to Workforce, Athletes, Teams, Partner Organizations and 32 
Community Members in priority order. For the purposes of this 33 
Agreement AWHU are classified as Dwelling Units.  34 
35 
1.5 Athlete Dormitory Dwelling Unit means Athlete and Workforce 36 
Housing Unit (AWHU). 37 
38 
Exhibit 4: Clean Proposed DA 4th Amended
111

conduct of winter sports training, hosting of competitions and athletic 41 
events and that comply with all other covenants and restrictions 42 
applicable to the Utah Olympic Park. 43 
 44 
1.7 Building Permit means a permit issued pursuant to the requirements of the 45 
Snyderville Basin Development Code, International Building Code and related 46 
building codes as applicable in the Snyderville Basin Planning District, including 47 
permits for grading, footings and foundations and construction of other 48 
improvements. 49

and programs at the Utah Olympic Park in Park City, Utah the Utah Olympic Oval 59 
in Kearns, Utah, and Soldier Hollow in Wasatch County, Utah. 60

amendments. 62

customarily associated with hotels and recreational lodging. Hotel/Lodging units 67 
may be professionally or independently branded, managed, or operated.  68

that certain Exchange Agreement, dated November 6,2013, attached hereto as 164 
Exhibit J, whereby various parcels of property have been exchanged between 165 
the entities. Such exchanges have enabled Developer to relocate portions of the 166 
proposed residential uses at the Project in order to, among other benefits, further 167 
minimize visibility to and from Kimball Junction. It is further agreed that such 168 
exchange will not alter the overall density rights for the Project. 169

Olympic Park SPA Plan Book of Exhibits which are incorporated by reference 171 
herein. 172

associated with the development and construction of the Project except, as to the 174 
performance of any additional obligations which shall be specified in this 175 
Development Agreement. 176

development locations as are permitted under this Development Agreement. 180

Commission. 183

FINDINGS 209 
The County Council of the County, acting in its legislative capacity, has made the 210 
following determinations with respect to the Olympic Park SPA Plan set forth in this 211 
Article 3, including all findings of fact and conclusions of law as are necessary to make 212 
each of such determinations: 213 
3.1 Following a lawfully advertised public hearing, Developer received a positive 214 
recommendation for amendments to this Development Agreement by action of 215 
the Planning Commission taken on _____________, 2025. 216 
3.2 The County Council held a lawfully advertised public hearing on ____________, 217 
2025, and during a lawfully advertised public meeting on that same date, 218 
approved this Amended Development Agreement under the processes and 219 
procedures set forth in the Code and General Plan. 220 
3.3 The terms and conditions of approval are incorporated fully into this 221 
Development Agreement. In making such approval, the County Council made 222 
such findings of fact and conclusions Of law as are required as a condition to the 223 
approvals, as reflected in the staff recommendation, as reflected in the minutes 224 
of the above referenced public meetings, and as reflected by the other 225 
enumerated findings herein. 226 
Exhibit 4: Clean Proposed DA 4th Amended
116
3.4 The Olympic Park SPA provides substantial, tangible benefits to the general 227 
public of the Snyderville Basin that significantly outweigh those that would be 228 
derived if the development occurred under the provisions of the existing zone. 229 
The provisions of those benefits and amenities have been taken into 230 
consideration by Summit County in granting increased residential and 231 
commercial densities on the Project. 232 
3.5 The Olympic Park SPA Plan, as reflected in and conditioned by the terms and 233 
conditions of the Development Agreement and the amendments thereto, is in 234 
conformity and compliance with the General Plan, any existing capital 235 
improvement programs, the provisions of the Code (including concurrency and 236 
infrastructure requirements), and all other development requirements of the 237 
County. 238 
3.6 The Olympic Park SPA Plan contains outstanding features which advance the 239 
policies, goals and objectives of the General Plan beyond mere conformity, 240 
including the following: (i) agreements with respect to design controls and 241 
limitations to minimize the visual impact of the Project; (ii) dedication and 242 
preservation of view shed and environmental features; (iii) taking advantageous 243 
use of natural depressions in topography to minimize the visual prominence and 244 
potential for ridge lining; (iv) appropriate location of density and uses; (v) the 245 
preservation of critical open space areas; (vi) the promotion of recreation uses 246 
and resort related Public Benefits that are appropriate to support the community 247 
and recreational nature of the area; (vii) contribution to community trails and 248 
parks including working with the Snyderville Basin Special Recreation District to 249 
identify possible trail linkages and trail heads; (viii) exceeding open space 250 
requirements for the Project; (ix) using efforts to minimize visibility of the 251 
residential housing to and from Kimball Junction; (x) provides incentives to 252 
private development partners to avoid depleting the endowment established 253 
to fund the operation of the venues operated by the Developer. 254 
3.7 There are unique circumstances that justify the use of a SPA, including 255 
preserving the long-term viability of the Olympic Park. County and Developer 256 
acknowledge that the existing facilities were built by the State of Utah without 257 
any zoning or building approvals. In order to preserve the long-term viability of 258 
the Olympic Park, additional funding is needed to avoid depleting the 259 
endowment established to fund the operation of the Olympic Park. Expanded 260 
commercial development within the Olympic Park SPA is one way to provide this 261 
funding. 262 
3.8 Developer has committed to comply with Affordable Housing Requirements 263 
consistent with other resort areas in Summit County and as delineated in Exhibit 264 
H herein. County acknowledges and agrees that to the extent that Developer 265 
exceeds the minimum unit equivalent Affordable Housing Requirements under 266 
Exhibit H, Developer may use such additional housing in any manner it sees fit 267 
consistent with the terms of this Agreement including  provisions for residential 268 
uses or Hotel, Inn, including, but not limited to, charging a higher rental rate and 269 
Exhibit 4: Clean Proposed DA 4th Amended
117
utilizing such housing as nightly rentals for visiting athletes, Olympic Park 270 
guests, or officials. 271 
3.9 Subject to the terms of this Development Agreement, Developer has committed 272 
to comply with all appropriate concurrency and infrastructure requirements of the 273 
Code, and all appropriate criteria and standards described in the Development 274 
Agreement and the amendments thereto, including any applicable impact fees of 275 
the County and its Special Districts (subject to Articles 6.1 and 6.2 below). 276 
3.10 There exist adequate provisions for mitigation of all fiscal and service impacts on 277 
the general public. 278 
3.11 Any construction management impacts shall be addressed in the Construction 279 
Management and Mitigation Plan established for the Olympic Park SPA and 280 
approved by the County Engineer, as required in Article 4.6 hereof. 281 
3.12 The approval of the Olympic Park SPA Plan will not adversely affect the public 282 
health, safety and general welfare of the residents of Summit County, it being 283 
acknowledged that the proposed Project will substantially enhance and benefit 284 
the public health, safety and general welfare of the residents of Summit County. 285 
3.13 The Olympic Park SPA Plan meets or exceeds development quality and 286 
aesthetic objectives of the General Plan and the Code, is consistent with the 287 
goal of orderly growth in the Snyderville Basin, and minimizes construction 288 
impacts on public infrastructure within the Snyderville Basin. 289 
3.14 The proposed development reasonably assures life and property within the 290 
Snyderville Basin is protected from any adverse impact of its development, it 291 
being acknowledged that the proposed Project will substantially enhance and 292 
benefit life and property within the Snyderville Basin. 293 
3.15 Developer shall take reasonably appropriate measures to prevent harm to 294 
neighboring properties and lands from development, including nuisances. 295 
3.16 Exemption from Code. The County Council acting pursuant to its authority under 296 
Utah Code Annotated 17-27a-101 et seq., as well as its regulations and 297 
guidelines, in the exercise of its legislative discretion, has determined that the 298 
Olympic Park SPA is exempt from the application of the Code solely to the extent 299 
that such a finding may be a condition precedent to approval of this 300 
Development Agreement. Where there is a direct conflict between an express 301 
provision of this Development Agreement and the Code or General Plan or other 302 
Land Use Laws, this Development Agreement shall take precedence; otherwise, 303 
the Code, General Plan, or other Land Use Laws shall control. 304

Article 5 503 
VESTED RIGHTS 504 
5.1 Vested Rights/Approved Use, Density, and Configuration. Subject to the terms, 505 
conditions and requirements of this Development Agreement, Developer is 506 
hereby vested with respect to the Olympic Park SPA as to the uses, densities, 507 
design guidelines and methods, development standards, the Final Site Plans 508 
and other approval processes, as reflected in the Olympic Park SPA Plan Book 509 
of Exhibits and all other provisions of this Development Agreement and 510 
amendments hereto. Subject to the conditions and requirements of this 511 
Development Agreement, Developer shall have the vested right to construct the 512 
Project in accordance with the terms and conditions of this Development 513 
Agreement and amendments hereto. 514 
5.2 Reserved Legislative Powers/Future Changes of Laws and Plans/Compelling 515 
Countervailing Public Interest. Nothing in this Development Agreement shall limit 516 
the future exercise of the police power of  the County in enacting zoning, 517 
subdivision, development, growth management, platting, environmental, open 518 
space, transportation and other master plans, policies, ordinances and 519 
regulations after the date of the Development Agreement. Notwithstanding the 520 
retained power of the County to enact such legislation under the police power, 521 
such legislation shall only be applied to modify the vested rights described in 522 
Article 5.1, as well as other provisions of this Development Agreement, based 523 
upon policies, facts and circumstances meeting the compelling, countervailing 524 
public interest exception to the vested rights doctrine in the State of Utah. 525 
(Western Land Equities Inc v. City of Logan, 617 P. 2d 388 (Utah 1980), U.C.A. 526 
17-27a-508, or other successor case and statutory law). Any such proposed 527 
change affecting the vested rights of Developer and other provisions of this 528 
Development Agreement shall be of general application to all development 529 
activity in the Snyderville Basin; and, unless the County declares an emergency, 530 
Developer shall be entitled to prior written notice and an opportunity to be heard 531 
with respect to the proposed change and its applicability to the Olympic Park 532 
under the compelling, countervailing public policy exception to the vested rights 533 
doctrine. In the event that the County does not give prior written notice, 534 
Developer shall retain the' right to be heard before an open meeting of the 535 
Exhibit 4: Clean Proposed DA 4th Amended
124
County Council in the event Developer alleges that its rights under this 536 
Development Agreement have been adversely affected.  537 
5.3 Normal Operating Capacity / Special Event Permits. County acknowledges and 538 
agrees that there is existing operating capacity within the Olympic Park to 539 
accommodate up to 2,000 people at any one point and therefore the facility is 540 
recognized for having that approved normal operating capacity. 541 
5.3.1 Accordingly, the County Council in its legislative capacity hereby waives 542 
the requirement under the Code that Developer apply for and obtain a 543 
special event permit for events and activities it is hosting within the 544 
Olympic Park for capacities of 2,000 people and less. Developer shall use 545 
every reasonable effort to encourage trip reduction including: carpool, 546 
transit, HOV, and non-motorized access. 547 
5.3.2 Developer shall ensure that at least one first aid station staffed and 548 
equipped with the minimum equipment and staffing level of at least two 549 
(2) state-licensed or certified medical providers, such as an emergency 550 
medical technician, paramedic, nurse, physician's assistant or medical 551 
doctor are present to staff each first aid station over the duration of the 552 
event. 553 
5.3.2.1 The first aid station(s) shall be clearly marked, identifiable and 554 
strategically located to provide expedient medical care for those 555 
attending or participating in the events at the Olympic Park. 556 
5.3.2.2 The first aid station(s) shall be easily accessible by emergency 557 
vehicles. The Developer shall provide the Park City Fire District 558 
(PCFD) with a map of the gathering site which includes the 559 
locations of first aid station(s), emergency vehicle ingress and 560 
egress routes, landing zones (if applicable) and rendezvous 561 
locations. 562 
5.3.2.3 Developer shall ensure that the staff person in charge of the first 563 
aid station(s) keeps accurate records of patients and treatment, 564 
and that the health officer is notified of all cases involving 565 
serious injury or communicable disease in accordance with 566 
R386-702 (Communicable Disease Rule) and R3 86-703 (Injury 567 
Reporting Rule). 568 
5.3.3 Temporary structures and/or facilities, such as tents, food vendor carts, 569 
etc., that are part of any such activities, shall be required to apply for and 570 
receive standard use permits as required by the County and the Park City 571 
Fire Special Service District.  572 
5.3.4 At the discretion of the County's Community Development Director, should 573 
the Olympic Park show repeated failures to satisfy local regulatory 574 
Exhibit 4: Clean Proposed DA 4th Amended
125
agencies (such as Health and Fire District), then the Community 575 
Development Director shall have the ability to require traditional special 576 
event permits of the Developer up until regular compliance has been met 577 
for any events under 2,000 people. 578 
5.3.5 Expected crowds greater than 2,000 people for any given event (i.e., an 579 
Olympic event) must comply with County regulations regarding the 580 
application for and the obtaining of a Special Event Permit for such event. 581 
5.4 Towers and Recreational Structures. Non-building structures at the Olympic Park 582 
SPA that are in excess of thirty-two (32) feet such as existing chairlift towers, 583 
adventure course towers and recreational towers and non-building structures of 584 
a similar type and nature shall be deemed support infrastructure and shall be 585 
deemed a conforming use. The Developer shall follow the permitting process as 586 
outlined in Exhibit C to obtain any appropriate permit from the County for future 587 
non-building structures. 588 
5.5 Interconnection. Developer supports and shall work in good faith to coordinate 589 
with the Master Developer of Canyons Resort and other parties involved, to 590 
make a physical transportation interconnect between Canyons Resort, Olympic 591 
Park, and Kimball Junction. Such an interconnect shall be reviewed through the 592 
Low Impact Permit approval process. 593

impact fee ordinance and implementing resolution. Notwithstanding the agreement of 616 
Developer to subject the Olympic Park SPA to impact fees under the above-stated 617 
conditions, Developer does not waive Developer' s rights under any applicable law to 618

6.2 Considerations for Community Benefits. The County acknowledges that 621 
Developer is a not-for-profit entity and will be supplying a substantial benefit to 622 
the community. In addition, it is a central purpose of the Project to preserve the 623 
long term viability of the Olympic Park by obtaining additional funding and to 624 
incent private development partners to avoid depleting the endowment 625 
established to fund the operation of the Olympic Park. Accordingly, the County 626 
agrees to reasonably consider, to the extent allowed by applicable law, the 627 
following avenues of potential assistance: 628 
6.3.1 Economic Development Tools such as Tax Increment Financing, , 629 
public infrastructure districts, special service district designations, creation 630 
of political subdivision of the State, or similar land designations that would 631 
assist the Utah Athletic Foundation in its long-term sustainability efforts. 632 
6.3.2 County merit-based grant allocations, such as or similar to Transient 633 
Room Tax (TRT) or resort communities sales and use tax, to assist the 634 
Utah Athletic Foundation in efforts to further enhance the outlined 635 
community benefits. 636 
6.4 Future Olympic Games In the event that, during the term of this Development 637 
Agreement, the State of Utah is chosen to host another Olympic Games and, as 638 
a result of such Olympic Games, the Olympic Park is identified as an official 639 
competition venue, the County will look to assist these efforts by: 640 
6.4.1 Allowing transportation and security staging areas in designated areas of 641 
property located below the Olympic Park and in the surrounding Kimball 642 
Junction area, as conceptually outlined in the Olympic Park SPA Plan 643 
Book of Exhibits as Exhibit I, subject to any terms and conditions deemed 644 
necessary by the County and the Snyderville Basin Special Recreation 645 
District together with any releases or waivers required by that certain 646 
Declaration and Notice of Use Restrictions, dated November 20, 2008, to 647 
ensure that any area of disturbance is immediately and effectively  648 
restored upon termination of the Olympic Games. 649 
6.4.2 Provide planning support and assistance to any bid / organizing committee 650 
efforts. 651 
6.4.3 Fairly negotiate any community services agreements and support staging 652 
a future effort. 653 
Exhibit 4: Clean Proposed DA 4th Amended
127
6.4.4 Designate the Utah Olympic Park as a “Major Sporting Event Venu” 654 
pursuant to SB333, 2025 General Legislative Session. 655 
6.5 Transportation Special Service District. When public bus service to the Olympic 656 
Park is made available by the County, Developer agrees to be annexed into the 657 
Kimball Area Transportation Special Service District and pay such assessments, 658 
on an equal basis, as are required of other property owners therein. 659 
6.6 Traffic Capacity. Developer's traffic report, Exhibit K. 1, acknowledged limitations 660 
in traffic capacity with the accessing area. Limitations for Phase I of the project, 661 
as identified in the traffic report, have been corrected with the cooperative efforts 662 
of Summit County and UDOT by implementing dual lefts at Olympic Park and 663 
SR-224. The Developer acknowledges the need for additional access capacity 664 
prior to construction of Phase 2 as identified in Exhibit K.1. The Snyderville Basin 665 
Transportation Master Plan identifies means to provide the required capacity. 666 
These concepts or others as amended in the Transportation Master Plan will 667 
need to be implemented in advance of Phase 2 construction. 668

Article 7  672 
PUBLIC BENEFITS 673 
7.1 Contribution of Public Benefits. As a consideration for the County's entry into the 674 
Development Agreement, Developer shall provide the following Public Benefits 675 
beyond those public impacts created by the Project: 676 
7.1.1 UAF Property Open Space Preservation. Developer shall preserve the 677 
natural open space areas owned by the Developer and shown on the 678 
Master Conceptual Development Plan in the same general condition as 679 
those areas are presently found. Any disturbance of those areas for 680 
construction staging or the installation of utility lines shall be restored 681 
within one (1) year of completion of said construction or installation. 682 
7.1.2 Trails. Developer shall contribute to community trails and parks including 683 
working with the Snyderville Basin Special Recreation District to identify 684 
possible trail linkages and trail heads. 685 
7.1.3 Recreational Programs. Provide affordable, subsidized long term 686 
community recreation programs (especially for Summit County's youth). 687 
7.1.4 Olympic Winter Sport. Provide unique public program offerings that 688 
highlight our region's support and interest in Olympic Winter Sports. 689 
Exhibit 4: Clean Proposed DA 4th Amended
128
7.1.5 Olympic Legacy Foundation. Provide local organizational leadership 690 
towards initiatives of Utah's Olympic Legacy Foundation that ties in 691 
statewide interests to Summit County. 692 
7.1.6 Education Outreach. The nature of the Utah Athletic Foundation (d.b.a. 693 
"Utah Olympic Legacy Foundation") is to promote an ongoing legacy 694 
of the Olympic movement's efforts, to include efforts to promote sport 695 
and physical activity for all (especially for the community's youth) and 696 
to promote educational curriculum and community partner programs 697 
that have similar interests. It is the intent of the leadership of the Utah 698 
Athletic Foundation to utilize the Olympic Park for numerous and 699 
diverse community meetings and educational components that will 700 
serve a broad regional and statewide interest. 701

Article 8 703 
SUCCESSORS AND ASSIGNS 704 
8.1 Binding Effect. This Development Agreement shall be binding on the successors 705 
and assigns of Developer in the ownership or development of any portion of the 706 
Olympic Park SPA. Notwithstanding the foregoing, a purchaser of the Project or 707 
any portion thereof shall be responsible for performance of Developer's 708 
obligations hereunder as to the portion of the Project so transferred in 709 
accordance with the provisions of Article 8.2 herein. 710 
8.2 Transfer of the Project. Developer shall be entitled to transfer any portion of the 711 
Project subject to the terms of the Development Agreement and any 712 
amendments upon written notice to the County. In the event of any such 713 
complete transfer of all or a portion of Developer's interests in the Project, the 714 
transferee shall be deemed to be Developer for all purposes under this 715 
Development Agreement with respect to that portion of the Project transferred. 716 
Developer's obligation to notify the County shall terminate with respect to 717 
portions of the Project on which all of the improvements required by this 718 
Development Agreement have been substantially completed as evidenced by a 719 
certificate of occupancy granted by the County. 720 
8.3 Release of Developer. In the event of a transfer of all or a portion of the Project, 721 
Developer shall obtain an assumption by the transferee of Developer's 722 
obligations under this Development Agreement, and, in such event, the 723 
transferee shall be fully substituted as Developer under the Development 724 
Agreement, and Developer shall be released from any further obligations with 725 
respect to this Development Agreement as to the parcel so transferred. 726 
8.4 Obligations and Rights of Mortgage Lenders. The holder of any mortgage, deed 727 
of trust, or other security arrangement with respect to the Project, or any portion 728 
Exhibit 4: Clean Proposed DA 4th Amended
129
thereof, shall not be obligated under this Development Agreement to construct or 729 
complete improvements or to guarantee such construction or completion, but 730 
shall otherwise be bound by all of the terms and conditions of this Development 731 
Agreement and any amendments which pertain to the Project or such portion 732 
thereof in which it holds an interest. Any such holder who comes into possession 733 
of the Project, or any portion thereof, pursuant to a foreclosure of a mortgage or 734 
a deed of trust, or deed in lieu of such foreclosure shall take the Project, or such 735 
portion thereof, subject to any pro rata claims for payments or charges against 736 
the Project, or such portion thereof, deed restrictions, or other obligations which 737 
accrue prior to the time such holder comes into possession. Nothing in this 738 
Development Agreement shall be deemed or• construed to permit or authorize 739 
any such holder to devote the Project, or any portion thereof, to any uses, or to 740 
construct any improvements thereon, other than those uses and improvements 741 
provided for or authorized by the Development Agreement and any 742 
amendments, and, as would be the case in any assignment, the purchaser of the 743 
Project from the holder shall be subject to all of the terms and conditions of the 744 
Development Agreement, including the obligation to complete all required 745 
amenities and improvements and to comply with all parking provisions. 746

Article 9 749 
DEFAULT, TERMINATION AND ARBITRATION 750 
9.1 Default. 751 
9.1.1 Events of Default. Default under this Development Agreement occurs 752 
upon the happening of one or more of the following events or conditions: 753 
9.1.1.1 A warranty, representation or statement made or furnished by 754 
Developer to the County in this Development Agreement, including 755 
any attachments hereto, is materially false or proves to have been 756 
materially false in any material respect when it was made. 757 
9.1.1.2 A finding and determination made by the Summit County Manager 758 
by substantial evidence that the Developer has not complied in 759 
good faith with one or more of the material terms or conditions of 760 
this Development Agreement. 761 
9.1.1.3 Any other event, condition, act or omission by Developer, which 762 
materially interferes with the intent and objective of this 763 
Development Agreement, after the expiration of all notice and cure 764 
periods. 765 
9.1.2 Notice and Cure of Default. Within ten (10) days after the occurrence of 766 
default, the County shall give Developer (the "defaulting party'.') written 767 
notice specifying the nature of the alleged default and, when appropriate, 768 
the manner in which the default must be satisfactorily cured. Developer 769 
shall have sixty (60) days after receipt of written notice to cure the default. 770 
After proper notice and expiration of the sixty (60) day cure period without 771 
cure, the County may terminate or amend this Development Agreement 772 
by giving written notice in accordance with the procedure adopted by the 773 
County. Failure or delay in giving notice of default shall not constitute a 774 
waiver of any default, nor shall it change the time of default. 775 
Notwithstanding the sixty (60) day cure period provided above, in the 776 
event more than thirty (30) days is reasonably required to cure a default 777 
and Developer, within the sixty (60) day cure period, commences actions 778 
reasonably designed to cure the default, then the cure period shall be 779 
extended for such additional period as Developer is prosecuting those 780 
actions diligently to completion. 781 
9.1.3 Non-Waiver.  Neither Party waives any claim of defect in performance of 782 
this Development Agreement by the other Party. 783 
Exhibit 4: Clean Proposed DA 4th Amended
131
9.1.4 Other Remedies. All other remedies at law or in equity which are 784 
consistent with the provisions of this Development Agreement are 785 
available to the Parties to pursue in the event there is a breach. 786 
9.2 Termination 787 
9.2.1 Termination Upon Repudiation. An express repudiation, refusal or 788 
renunciation of this Development Agreement, if the same is in writing and 789 
signed by the Developer, shall be sufficient to terminate this Development 790 
Agreement and a public hearing on the matter shall not be required. 791 
9.2.2 Termination Upon Completion of Development. This Development 792 
Agreement shall terminate when the Project has been fully developed and 793 
Developer's and the County's obligations in connection therewith are 794 
satisfied, or at the expiration of the term of this Development Agreement 795 
as set forth in Article 10.5. The County shall record a notice that this 796 
Development Agreement has been fully performed and therefore has 797 
been terminated. 798 
9.2.3 Effect of Termination on Developer Obligations. Termination of the 799 
Development Agreement as to any Developer of the Project or any portion 800 
thereof shall not affect any such Developer's obligations under this 801 
Agreement or obligations to comply with the terms and conditions of any 802 
applicable zoning, or subdivision plat, site plan, Building Permit, or other 803 
land use entitlements approved with respect to the Project, nor shall it 804 
affect any other covenants or any other development requirements 805 
specified or created pursuant to the Development Agreement. Termination 806 
of the Development Agreement shall not affect or invalidate in any manner 807 
Developer's obligations of mutual release under Article 10.8 or 808 
indemnification and hold harmless under Article 10.16. 809 
9.2.4 Effect of Termination on the County Obligations. Upon any termination of 810 
the Development Agreement, the entitlements (including density), 811 
conditions of development, limitations on fees, and all other terms and 812 
conditions of the Development Agreement and any amendments shall no 813 
longer be vested by reason of the Development Agreement with respect 814 
to any portion of the Project then undeveloped and not then covered by a 815 
Building Permit application. Those undeveloped portions of the Project 816 
may be subject to then existing planning and zoning law. Upon such a 817 
termination, the County shall no longer be prohibited by the Development 818 
Agreement from making any changes or modifications to such 819 
entitlements, conditions, or fees applicable to such undeveloped portions 820 
of the Project. Further, with respect to the improved portions of the 821 
Project, the County shall remain obligated to recognize and apply the 822 
development standards and configuration contained in the Olympic Park 823 
SPA Plan Book of Exhibits. 824

GENERAL TERMS AND CONDITIONS 832 
10.1 No Addition to Project. No property may be added to the Project or to the 833 
Olympic Park SPA for purposes of the Development Agreement, except by 834 
written amendment. This Development Agreement shall not affect any land other 835 
than the Project. 836 
10.2 Development Agreement to run with the Land. The Development Agreement and 837 
any amendments shall be recorded against the Olympic Park. The agreements 838 
contained herein shall be deemed to run with the land and shall be binding on 839 
and shall inure to the benefit of all successors in ownership of the Project. 840 
10.3 Construction of Development Agreement. The Development Agreement shall be 841 
construed so as to effectuate the public purpose of resolving disputes, 842 
implementing long-range planning objectives, obtaining public benefits, and 843 
protecting any compelling, countervailing public interest, while providing 844 
assurances of continued vested development rights under the Development 845 
Agreement. 846 
10.4 Laws of General Applicability. Where the Development Agreement refers to laws 847 
of general applicability to the Olympic Park SPA and other properties, that 848 
language shall be deemed to refer to laws, which apply to, all other developed 849 
and subdivided properties within the Snyderville Basin of Summit County. 850 
10.5 Duration. The term of this Development Agreement shall commence on, and the 851 
effective date of this Development Agreement shall be, the effective date of the 852 
Ordinance approving this Development Agreement. The initial term shall be for a 853 
ten (10) year period. Prior to the expiration of any ten (10) year term, the 854 
Developer may request in writing an additional ten (I 0) year extension of this 855 
Development Agreement from the County Council. The written request may be 856 
made no more than two (2) times for a total duration of thirty (30) years if both 857 
extensions are granted. The County Council shall review the extension 858 
request(s) to ensure that the Developer is in compliance with this Development 859 
Agreement and that the request does not jeopardize the health, safety and 860 
welfare of the citizens of Summit County. Absent any negative determination 861 
from the County Council in the course of the above described review, the County 862 
Council shall approve the extension request. At the expiration of this 863 
Development Agreement, or the renewal period, the Olympic Park shall become 864

subject to the then existing Development Code and General Plan and all 865 
development rights vested under this Development Agreement shall expire. 866

STAFF REPORT
To:   Snyderville Basin Planning Commission   
From:   Jennifer Leslie  
Date of Meeting: August 26, 2025
Date of Report:  August 20, 2025
Type of Item:  Conditional Use Permit - P ublic Hearing, Possible Action
Process:  Administrative Review
File #:   25-067
Proposal 
The applicant proposes to construct a 5,710 sf Major Accessory Building on Parcel PP-87-8. The 
Major Accessory Building will be 26’ in height and will be used exclusively by the owners of the 
property as a garage and storage space. The proposed Major Accessory Building shall be 
constructed in the same location as an existing Major Accessory Building that will be 
demolished. 
Project Description
Project Name:    Wright Accessory Building
Applicant(s):    Rosemary Stum, Lloyd Architecture
Property Owner(s):   Nathan Wright
Location:    4865 N 400 W, Park City
Zone District:     Rural Residential (RR)
Parcel Number and Size:   PP-87-8 (4.80 acres)
Type of Process:   Administrative
Final Land Use Authority:  Snyderville Basin Planning Commission (SBPC)
Vicinity Map
142
2 
 
Background 
Parcel PP-87-8 is a 4.80-acre property in the Old Ranch Road neighborhood accessed from 400 
West, a private road. The property is not within a platted subdivision.  
 
The property is currently developed with a single-family dwelling (SFD) built in 1969. The SFD is 
approximately 4,253 sqft with attached garage space. The property is also developed with 
multiple accessory structures including two Minor Accessory Buildings, one Secondary 
Accessory Building, and one Major Accessory Building that is used as a barn. The barn has its 
own driveway area and has a footprint of approximately 15,800 sf.  
 
The applicant intends to demolish all the existing structures on the property and rebuild a new 
SFD, Minor Accessory, and Major Accessory Building. A Low Impact Permit (LIP) was approved 
by the Community Development Director on July 21, 2025 for the new SFD on a lot outside of a 
platted subdivision. The LIP also approved Private Recreation uses as shown in the site plan, 
which includes an outdoor sport court and swimming pool. 
 
The proposed Major Accessory Building is 5,710 sqft and 26’ high. The building will be used 
exclusively by the owners of the property as a garage and storage space. The proposed Major 
Accessory is positioned in the same location as the existing Major Accessory Building that will 
be demolished. The Code establishes that Major Accessory Buildings are conditional uses in the 
RR zone. 
 
Analysis and Findings 
Chapter 2 - Zone District Requirements 
The subject property is located in the Rural Residential (RR) zone district.

Summit County Recorder (Entry No. 256012) between August 1, 1977 and January 14, 1993,

been no changes to the legal description since the deed was recorded.

Conditional Use Permit Criteria for Approval 
No conditional use permit shall be approved unless the applicant demonstrates that: 
  
Standard 1: The use is in accordance with the General Plan. 
  
Analysis: Chapter 2 of the Code conforms to the General Plan, as stated in Section 10-2-
16. Chapter 2 lists “Major Accessory Buildings” as conditional uses, therefore, the 
proposal is compatible with the General Plan. Code amendments adopted via Ordinance 
930 on September 8, 2021 updated the criteria for Accessory Buildings. The 
amendments were specifically passed to preserve the natural, scenic, and historic 
qualities of the Snyderville Basin, which is directly supportive of goals within the General 
Plan.   
 
Standard 2: The use conforms to all applicable provisions of this Title, including, but not limited 
to, any applicable provisions of this Section and Chapter 4 of this Title, the General Plan, and 
State and Federal regulations;

Standard 3: The use is not detrimental to public health, safety and welfare; COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: The plans for the proposed Major Accessory Building and SFD have been 
reviewed by the Summit County Sherrif’s Office, the Health, Building, and Engineering 
Departments and no concerns were raised regarding the proposed uses to public health, 
safety, and welfare.  
 
Standard 4: The use is appropriately located with respect to public facilities; COMPLIES 
   
Analysis: The proposed property is near existing road and public safety facilities within 
an established neighborhood area within the Snyderville Basin. The property has been 
developed with a SFD since 1969 and has had sufficient access to public facilities.  
 
Standard 5: The use is compatible with the existing neighborhood character and with the 
character and purpose provision of the applicable zoning district and will not adversely affect 
surrounding land uses. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: The Snyderville Basin General Plan establishes that development in the Old 
Ranch Road neighborhood should “compatible with the large lot single family detached 
dwellings and equestrian uses”. The proposed style and types of uses are consistent 
with nearby development within the neighborhood and the RR zone district.

Public Notice, Meetings and Comments  
This application was publicly noticed as a public hearing with possible action with the SBPC for 
the August 12, 2025 meeting. Notice was physically posted and published online prior to the 
meeting. Courtesy postcards were mailed to property owners within 1000’ of the subject 
property. The item was published in the Park Record. Staff made an error with the address 
published on the agenda for the August 12th meeting; therefore, the project was moved to the 
next available meeting (August 26, 2025). New postcards were mailed to neighbors to provide 
notice of the meeting date change. 
 
As of the date of this report, no public comment was received by Staff.  
 
Recommendation 
Staff recommends that the Snyderville Basin Pl anning Commission (SBPC) review the proposal, 
conduct a public hearing, and a pprove the application, pursuant to the following findings of 
fact, conclusions of law, and conditions of approval. 
 
Findings of Fact: 
1. On April 25, 2025, the Summit County Community Development Department received a 
complete application for a Conditional Use Permit (CUP) for a Major Accessory Building 
at 4865 N 400 W, Park City (the property).  
2. The property is identified as Parcel PP-87-8 and is 4.80 acres.  
3. The property is a Lot of Record in the Rural Residential (RR) zone.  
4. According to Summit County records, the property is owned by Nathan H Wright 
Trustee. Nathan Wright signed the CUP application form.  
5. The property is currently developed with a single-family dwelling (SFD), a large 
accessory building, and multiple smaller accessory buildings.  
6. The intent of this proposal is to permit a new Major Accessory Building on the property. 
All existing structures on-site will be removed and demolished.  
7. Although the existing dwelling is proposed to be demolished, a new SFD was reviewed 
and approved on July 21, 2025 by the Community Development Director through Low 
Impact Permit #25-133.  
8. As conditioned, prior to construction of the proposed Major Accessory Building, there 
shall be no other accessory structures on the lot.  
9. The height of the proposed Major Accessory Building is 26’.  
10. The footprint of the proposed building is 5,710 sf, which is under the maximum 
footprint size allowed by the lot size. 
11. The setbacks of the proposed Major Accessory Building are as follows: front = 150.75’, 
north side = 45’, south side = 314.33’, rear = 12’. 
12. The Major Accessory Building will be used as garage and storage space for the property 
owners. The Major Accessory Building is not intended for public use.  
151
11 
 
13. On-site privacy for adjacent land uses will not be affected by this proposal.  
14. Neither the design/siding/roof materials of the proposed SFD nor Major Accessory 
Building are prohibited design styles. The North and South facades are approximately 
78’ long. There is a façade shift that cumulatively is greater than 10’ combined with 
horizontal and vertical shifts. No greater than 75% of the façade length appears 
unbroken.  
15. The building identified as a pool house on the site plan is considered a Minor Accessory 
Building. The Minor Accessory is 354 sf, 20’ in height, and is less than 75% of the building 
footprint of the SFD approved in LIP #25-133. 
16. The proposal will not combine accessory structures. There are no signs proposed.  
17. The principal use of the lot is residential and has been used as a residence. The 
proposed new SFD will be constructed simultaneously.   
18. The project will not produce emissions that conflict with the State’s air quality 
standards. The proposal does not include any point-source discharge. The Summit 
County Stormwater Department reviewed the proposal and did not raise concerns 
regarding non-point discharge or water runoff concerns. The proposal is not within 
1,500’ above or 100’ below any springs used for culinary or public water.  
19. No sensitive soils, critical areas, or watercourses are on site and the proposal will not 
adversely affect critical wildlife habitat. There are no proposed roads.  
20. The proposal will not encroach into any wetlands, slopes exceeding 30%, ridgelines, 
avalanche zones, or geologic hazard areas.  
21. This project complies with the base zone density and is therefore exempt from open 
space requirements. 
22. The proposal is not within 300’ of an existing SBWRD line. The project will be served by a 
septic system. The applicant submitted passing percolation test results to the Summit 
County Health Department. 
23. There are more than two parking spaces available on site, including within the attached 
garage to the SFD and within the Major Accessory Building. 
24. The maximum grade of this driveway does not exceed 10% and is a minimum of 16ft 
wide. It only serves 1 dwelling.   
25. There is sufficient space within the garages to completely enclose the solid waste and 
recycling.  
26. There is sufficient space for snow removal and storage locations shown on the site plan.  
27. The property contains an existing well. The Water Right (35-14492) for the well includes 
sufficient culinary water for a residence and accessory unit, plus .09 acre feet of outdoor 
water. The Water Right includes sufficient water for all of the proposed uses on the 
property. 
28. All lighting for the Accessory Building and the SFD approved via LIP #25-133 are full 
cutoff fixtures that are downward directed and fully opaque so that light only escapes 
from the bottom of the fixture. The total outdoor light output is approximately 10,000 
lumens, which includes the lighting on the Accessory Building and the SFD. Soffit and 
wall mounted lights are mounted at between 8’ and 9’ 3” from finished grade. 
29. Staff and service providers reviewed the proposal.  
 
152
12 
 
Conclusions of Law:  
1. The use is in accordance with the general plan. 
2. The use conforms to all applicable provisions of the Code, including, but not limited to, 
any applicable provisions of Section 10-3-5, Chapter 4, and state and federal regulations. 
3. The use is not detrimental to public health, safety, and welfare. 
4. The use is appropriately located with respect to public facilities. 
5. The use is compatible with the existing neighborhood character and with the purpose 
provisions of the applicable zone district, and will not affect surrounding land uses. 
 
Conditions of Approval: 
1. A demolition permit must be obtained and all existing Accessory Buildings shall be 
removed from the site prior to approval of a Building Permit for the proposed Major 
Accessory Building,  
2. The SFD shall receive a CO prior to a final inspection of the Major Accessory Building.  
3. The improvements shall be as shown in Exhibits A and B.  
4. The Major Accessory Building is for the private use of the property owners and not for 
any commercial uses. Amendments to this permit are required to accommodate any 
proposed commercial activities.  
5. Any modifications to this permit shall be done in accordance with Section 10-3-5 I of the 
Snyderville Basin Development Code.  
6. The Major Accessory Building shall not be sold separately from the primary residence on 
the property.  
7. All lighting on the accessory building shall be done in accordance with the lighting 
submitted with this application and shown in Exhibit C. Any deviations from the lighting 
in Exhibit C shall comply with all lighting requirements in the Snyderville Basin 
Development Code.  
8. Prior to building permit issuance, all service provider requirements shall be satisfied.  
9. All necessary approvals and permits must be obtained, and fees paid, prior to 
recommencing any construction activity including but not limited to the County Health 
Department, Park City Fire District, County Engineer, County Building and County 
Planning Departments. 
10. This Conditional Use Permit shall expire one year from the date of approval unless the 
conditional use permit activity has begun, or an extension is requested in writing by the 
applicant and granted by the Community Development Director.

STAFF REPORT
To: Snyderville Basin Planning Commission
From: Jennifer Leslie, County Planner
Date of Meeting: August 26, 2025
Date of Report: August 20, 2025
Type of Item: Plat Amendment - Public Hearing, Possible Action
Process: Administrative Review
File #: 25-131
Proposal
The applicant proposes to move the southernmost building envelope on Lot 303A of the 
Timberline No. 3 Subdivision by approximately 30 ft north and 20 ft east. The proposed building 
envelope is 1,548 sf, which is the same size as the existing envelope. 
Project Description
Project Name: Timberline No. 3 Subdivision Lot 303A Amendment 
Applicant(s): Tyler York
Property Owner(s): Bitchby LLC
Location: 4922 Ponderosa Dr
Zone District: Hillside Stewardship (HS)
Parcel Number and Size: TL-3-A-303A-AM (1.36 acres)
Final Land Use Authority: Snyderville Basin Planning Commission (SBPC)
Vicinity Map
160
2 
 
Background 
The Timberline No. 3 Amended Subdivision includes twenty-three (23) lots and was recorded on 
in 1973. Lots 303, 304, 305, 306, and 307 were together amended as the Amended Plat of Lots 
303, 304, 305, 306, & 307, Timberline Subdivision Plat No.3 Amended in 2003. The 2003 
amendment added building envelopes to the applicable Lots.   
 
Lot 303A contains two building envelopes. The southernmost envelope is approximately 1,580 
sf. The northernmost envelope is approximately 22,295 sf and is developed with a single-family 
dwelling built in 2015.  
 
Tyler York, the Registered Agent of Bitchby LLC, the property owner, requests to adjust the 
southernmost building envelope on Lot 303A. The proposed amendment will move the 
envelope approximately 30 ft north and 20 ft east of its current placement. The proposed 
envelope is approximately 1548 sf. The applicant provided topography for the site. The 
proposed amendment would not move the building envelope into any slopes in excess of 30% 
or other critical areas. The building on the proposed envelope will be able to comply with all 
required setbacks.  
 
The proposed amendment will accommodate a future accessory structure on the property. 
Without the amendment, any structure placed within the envelope will interfere with driveway 
access to the dwelling. 
 
Staff and service providers reviewed the application and found that, as conditioned, the 
proposal complies with all applicable standards.  
 
Analysis and Findings 
Section 10-3-18 (I) of the Snyderville Basin Development Code sets the following grounds for 
vacating or changing a plat: 
 
Standard 1: If the county manager or land use authority is satisfied that neither the public nor 
any person will be materially injured by the proposed vacation, alteration or amendment, and 
that there is good cause for the vacation, alteration, or amendment, the county manager or 
land use authority may vacate, alter or amend the plat, any portion of the plat, or any road or 
lot. 
  
Analysis: The requested amendment would allow future construction on the property to 
occur outside of an established driveway that provides access to a single-family 
dwelling. The applicant has stated that they intend to construct and accessory dwelling 
on the southernmost building envelope. Accessory buildings are allowed and/or 
permitted uses in the Hillside Stewardship zone. Staff and service providers reviewed 
the proposal and raised no concerns. Staff finds no evidence that the public nor any 
person would be materially injured by the amendment. COMPLIES 
161
3 
 
 
Standard 2: No plat amendment shall be approved which results in an increase in density. 
 
Analysis: Density is defined in the Code as, “The number of dwelling units per acre with 
respect to residential land uses.” The applicant is requesting to amend the location of a 
building envelope on an existing residential lot. The amendment would not result in an 
additional residential unit. An existing single-family dwelling exists on the property. 
COMPLIES 
 
Public Notice, Meetings and Comments  
This item was noticed as a public hearing with possible action by the Snyderville Basin Planning 
Commission. The notice was physically posted on County property, published in The Park 
Record, and mailed to nearby land owners within 1,000’ from the property boundaries.  
 
As of the date of this report, no public comment has been received. 
 
Recommendation 
Staff recommends that the SBPC review the proposed plat amendment, conduct a public 
hearing, and approve the application, pursuant to the following Findings of Fact, Conclusions of 
Law, and Conditions of Approval. 
 
Findings of Fact: 
1. The Summit County Community Development Department received a complete 
application for a subdivision plat amendment for the Lot 303A of the Timberline No 3 
Subdivision on July 25, 2025.  
2. Lot 303A of the Timberline No. 3 Subdivision is identified as Parcel TL-3-A-303A-AM (the 
property) and is located at 4922 W Ponderosa Dr.  
3. The property is owned by Bitchby LLC. Tyler York, the Registered Agent of Bitchby LLC, 
signed the application form as the property owner and applicant.  
4. The property is in the Hillside Stewardship (HS) Zone and is 5.05 acres.  
5. The property is in the Timberline No. 3 Amended Subdivision, which was recorded on 
March 29, 1973 (Entry No. 119198).  
6. Lots 303, 304, 305, 306, and 307 were together amended as the Amended Plat of Lots 
303, 304, 305, 306, & 307, Timberline Subdivision Plat No.3 Amended on March 17, 
2003 (Entry No. 119198). 
7. The property includes two building envelopes as shown on the subdivision plat. The 
southernmost most building envelope is approximately 1,584 sf. The northernmost 
envelope is approximately 22,295 sf and is developed with a single-family dwelling built 
in 2015. 
8. The intent of this application is to adjust the southernmost building envelope so that a 
future building does not conflict with the existing driveway on-site.  
162
4 
 
9. The proposed amendment will move the building envelope approximately 30 ft north 
and 20 ft feet east of its current placement. The proposed size approximately is 1,548 sf.  
10. The proposed amendment would not move the building envelope into any slopes in 
excess of 30% or other critical areas. 
11. Density is defined in the Snyderville Basin Development Code as, “The number of 
dwelling units per acre with respect to residential land uses.”  The amendment would 
not result in any additional density. 
 
Conclusions of Law:  
1. Neither the public nor any person will be materially injured by the proposed 
amendment. 
2. The amendment would not result in an additional residential unit. 
 
Conditions of Approval: 
1. All service provider requirements shall be satisfied. 
2. Prior to issuance of a building permit, the amended plat shall be recorded in the Office 
of the Summit County Recorder.
