STAFF REPORT
To: Snyderville Basin Planning Commission
From: Jennifer Leslie, County Planner
Date of Meeting: August 26, 2025
Date of Report: August 20, 2025
Type of Item: Conditional Use Permit (CUP)
Process: Administrative Review
File #: 24-128
Project Description
Project Name: Altitude Lofts, Unit  3 – Accessory Dwelling Unit (ADU)
Applicant(s): Molly Guinan, Elliott Workgroup
Property Owner(s): Altitude 3 LLC
Location: 4123 N Forestdale Drive, #3
Zone District: Service Commercial (SC)
Parcel Number: ALTLC-3
Type of Item: Conditi onal Use Permit (CUP)
Process: Administrative Review
Final Land Use Authority: Snyderville Basin Planning Commission (SBPC)
Proposal
The applicant, on behalf of the owner, is requesting Conditional Use approval for an Accessory 
Dwelling Unit (ADU) in Unit 3 of the Altitude Lofts Condominiums. The applicant states that the 
three-level unit will include a ground floor garage, second-floor offices and conference area, 
and third floor ADU that is 999sf. 
Vicinity Map
166
2 
 
Background 
On August 15, 2024, the applicant submitted a complete application for a Conditional Use 
Permit (CUP) application to construct an Accessory Dwelling Unit (ADU) within the third floor of 
Unit 3 of the Altitude Loft Condominiums, located at 4123 Forestdale Dr Unit #3. The Altitude 
Loft Condominium plat was recorded on September 8, 2022 (Entry No. 1194835) after two shell 
buildings (Buildings A and B) were approved through a Low Impact Permit on May 26, 2022 (File 
#21-178). The Altitude Loft Condominiums are subject to the Park City Business Center 
Development Agreement and associated Parking Management Plan (Entry No. 1190794).  
 
The proposal for Unit 3 will include space for garage on the ground floor, office space and 
conference room on the second floor, and a 999 sf ADU on the third floor. The ADU will be 
accessed via an interior staircase that can also access business space. Exhibit B includes the 
proposed floor plans. The Code typically requires that ADU Floor Area calculations include 
staircase square footage. However, the Community Development Director (CDD) confirmed 
that because of the shared access to business space on the third floor, the staircase should not 
be calculated.   
 
Section 10-2-10 of the Snyderville Basin Development Code lists ADUs as a Conditional Use 
within the Service Commercial Zone. Only one (1) ADU may be permitted per commercial unit 
within the zone. Staff and service providers have reviewed the project and found that it 
complies, as conditioned, with all requirements of the Code and the Park City Business Center 
Development Agreement.  
 
Analysis and Findings 
Chapter 2 - Zone District Requirements 
The subject property is located in the Service Commercial zone district and is within the Lower 
Silver Creek Soils Overlay Zone.   
 
CODE REQUIREMENT ANALYSIS FINDING 
SC Zone District Intent 
The purpose of the service 
commercial/light industrial (SC) zone 
district is to provide appropriate 
locations within the Snyderville Basin 
for service commercial and light 
industrial uses, recognizing that such 
uses provide vital support functions for 
the basin's resort and residential 
economy. 
The proposed business will be 
Addison Holdings LLC., a 
personal financing business, 
which would fall within the 
intended commercial category 
of uses. Per Condition of 
Approval #7, the accompanying 
ADU will be used to provide 
housing for an employee of the 
business.

Conditional Use Permit Criteria  
No conditional use permit shall be approved unless the applicant demonstrates that: 
  
Standard 1: The use is in accordance with the General Plan. 
  
Analysis: Policy 2.16 of the Snyderville Basin General Plan states that new commercial 
developments should be located in existing commercial areas. This proposal supports 
that policy as Altitude Lofts is surrounded by other existing commercial uses in the 
Service Commercial Zone. Additionally, Policy 2.40 states that there should be a variety 
of housing types within the Snyderville Basin. The proposed ADU complies with that 
policy. COMPLIES

to, any applicable provisions of this Section and Chapter 4 of this Title, the General Plan, and 
State and Federal regulations;

Public Notice, Meetings and Comments  
Notice for a public hearing for this amendment to the Conditional Use Permit (CUP) was 
published in the Park Record and on public notice websites. Courtesy postcards were sent to all 
174
10 
 
property owners within 1,000 feet of the subject property. As of the date of this Staff Report, 
no public comment has been received. 
 
Recommendation 
Staff recommends that the Snyderville Basin Pl anning Commission (SBPC) review the proposed 
Conditional Use Permit, conduct a public hearing, and approve th e Conditional Use Permit for 
an Accessory Dwelling Unit (ADU) in Altitude Loft s Unit 3, pursuant to the following Findings of 
Fact, Conclusions of Law, and Conditions of Approval. 
    
Findings of Fact: 
1. On August 15, 2024, the applicant submitted a complete application for a Conditional 
Use Permit (CUP) application to construct an Accessory Dwelling Unit (ADU) within the 
third floor of Unit 3 of the Altitude Loft Condominiums.  
2. Unit 3 of the Altitude Loft Condominiums is owned by Altitude 3 LLC. Jamie Fraudenberg  
is the manager of the Altitude 3LLC and has signed the application form as the property 
owner.  
3. Unit 3 of the Altitude Loft Condominiums is considered Parcel ALTLC-3 and is located at 
4123 Forestdale Dr Unit #3. 
4. The Altitude Loft Condominiums were recorded on September 8, 2022 (Entry No. 
1194835) after two shell buildings (Buildings A and B) were approved through a Low 
Impact Permit on May 26, 2022 (File #21-178).  
5. Unit 3 is located within a shell building (Building A).  
6. The Altitude Loft Condominiums are subject to the Park City Business Center 
Development Agreement and associated Parking Management Plan (Entry No. 
1190794).  
7. The property is in the Service Commercial Zone and the Lower Silver Creek Soils Overlay 
Zone. 
8. Section 10-2-10 of the Snyderville Basin Development Code lists Accessory Dwelling 
Units (ADUs) as a Conditional Use within the Service Commercial Zone.  
9. Section 10-8-5 clarifies that ADUs shall be measured per the definition of Floor Area, 
which measures from the inside face of the perimeter wall to the inside face of the 
perimeter wall, including all wall partitions, mechanical rooms, hallways, and stairwells.  
10. The proposed ADU consists of 999 sf as measured per the definition of Floor Area.  
11. The proposal for Unit 3 will include space for garage on the ground floor, office space 
and conference room on the second floor, and an ADU on the third floor.  
12. The ADU will be accessed via a staircase from the second floor. Exhibit B includes the 
floor plans for this proposal.  
13. The Community Development Director determined that the staircase should not be 
counted toward the Floor Area for Unit 3 as the staircase also provides access to 
business space (Exhibit E).  
14. 
Only one ADU is proposed within the Unit 3.  
15. The proposed business that will occupy Unit 3 is Addison Holdings LLC., a personal 
financing business. Addison Holdings LLC has an existing Summit County Business 
License for a different location (Licencse #4497).  
175
11 
 
16. One full-time employee and two part-time employees are anticipated for the proposed 
business use.  
17. Parking for Altitude Loft Condominiums was approved in accordance with a Parking 
Management Agreement, which states that the Condominium Owner’s Association 
(COA) must review and attest to adequate parking for the uses proposed in each Unit 
prior to tenant improvements. The COA has provided a letter of attestation, attached as 
Exhibit D. 
18. Staff and service providers have reviewed the project and found that it complies, as 
conditioned, with all requirements of the Code and the Park City Business Center 
Development Agreement.  
 
Conclusions of Law:  
1. The use is in accordance with the Snyderville Basin General Plan. 
2. The use conforms to all applicable provisions of the Code, including, but not limited to, 
any applicable provisions of Section 10-3-5, Chapter 4, and state and federal regulations. 
3. The use is not detrimental to public health, safety, and welfare. 
4. The use is appropriately located with respect to public facilities. 
5. The use is compatible with the existing neighborhood character and with the purpose 
provisions of the applicable zone district and will not affect surrounding land uses. 
6. The use is in conforms to applicable provisions of the Park City Business Center 
Development Agreement.  
 
Conditions of Approval: 
1. The construction of the Accessory Dwelling Unit must be done in accordance with the 
plans included in Exhibit B. No modification to the Accessory Dwelling Unit is permitted 
without written approval from the Community Development Department and any 
relevant service providers.  
2. The third floor has been approved for residential use as an Accessory Dwelling Unit.  No 
additional portion of the unit may be used for residential purposes.   
3. At least one of the reserved parking spaces for Unit 3 must be reserved for the tenant of 
the Accessory Dwelling Unit.  
4. Verification of business operation and Business License must be submitted prior to the 
Certificate of Occupancy for the Accessory Dwelling Unit. 
5. A certificate of occupancy for the Accessory Dwelling Unit shall only be granted 
concurrent with or after the issuance of a certificate of occupancy for the associated 
commercial use. The Accessory Dwelling Unit may not be occupied prior to the 
associated commercial use receiving all necessary permits and approvals from service 
providers.  
6. The Accessory Dwelling Unit may not be sold separately from the associated commercial 
use.  
7.
 The Accessory Dwelling Unit may not be rented by anyone without affiliation to the 
associated commercial use. At least one (1) resident of the Accessory Dwelling Unit 
must be either the owner or employee of the associated business.  
176
12 
8. Nightly or short-term rental of the Accessory Dwelling Unit is prohibited. The rental
term of the Accessory Dwelling Unit shall be no less than thirty (30) days.
9. Loading and unloading of materials related to the commercial use may not interfere
with traffic circulation.
10. Prior to building permit issuance for this tenant improvement project, all service
provider requirements shall be satisfied.

PARKING MANAGEMENT AGREEMENT
(AltitudeLofts)
Summit County, Utah, a Utah politicalsubdivision,("County"), and Altitude Lofts, LLC,
a Utah limitedliabilitycompany ("Owner"), t intothisParking Management Agreement (this
"Agreement") as of this IW day of 2022, and agree as setforth below. The County
and the Owner arejointlyreferredto as the "Parties." Each partymay be referred to as a"Party."
RECITALS
WHEREAS, Owner has appliedfor a Low Impact Permit to develop Lot __ of the Park
City Business Center,4123 N. Forestdale Drive,more particularlydescribedin ExhibitA (the
"Property"),as an eleven (1l) unitcommercial condominium project ("Units"or individually,
"Unit");
WHEREAS, the Park City Business Center is govemed by restrictivecovenants,which
limit theallowed uses within the businesscenterto those thatdo not result in an excessive
demand for parking,do not resultin traffic orparking patternsthatobstructpedestrianor
vehicular access or obstructForestdaleDrive;
WHEREAS, despitethe Park City Business Center covenants,County isaware of
development and uses within the Park City Business Center that generatean excessivedemand
forparking compared to surfaceparking provided withinthe Property and that cause spillover
parking onto ForestdaleDrive;
WHEREAS, while County isnot a party to the Park City Business Center covenants and

STAFF REPORT
To:   Snyderville Basin Planning Commission   
From:   Jennifer Leslie, County Planner
Date of Meeting: August 26, 2025
Date of Report: August 20, 2025
Type of Item:  Low Impact Permit - Public He aring, Possible Recommendation 
Process:  Administrative Review
File #:   25-127
Proposal 
The applicant proposes to construct a 5,322 sf office building on Lot 9 on the Quarry Junction at 
Pinebrook Subdivision. The building will be a 27’ commercial shell with two units. The uses of 
each unit may vary with future tenants, but must comply with the approved uses in the 
Gorgoza Pines Consent Agreement. 
Project Description
Project Name:    Quarry Village  Lot 9 Commercial Shell Building
Applicant(s):    Chad Bollman, J. Ford Construction
Property Owner(s):   RJ Ford Properties LLC
Location:    3246 Santa Fe Road
Zone District:     Rural Residential (RR)
Parcel Number and Size:   QJPB-A-9-1AM (0.57 acres)
Type of Process:   Administrative
Final Land Use Authority:  Co mmunity Development Director
Vicinity Map
192
2 
 
Background 
The Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision was approved on May 21, 2004 and consists of 
eleven lots. The subdivision was created in accordance with the Gorgoza Pines Consent 
Agreement, which was approved by the Summit County Board of Commissioners on February 
24, 1997. The subdivision was approved for approximately 170,000 square feet of commercial 
density. Several buildings in the subdivision have been constructed or permits have been 
approved. The Quarry Village project was a master planned project and all infrastructure, 
including parking, was constructed when the project was approved.  
 
The Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision designates maximum gross square footages and 
maximum gross building footprints for each lot. Lot 9 is allowed 5,462 square feet of max gross 
building area and 5,462 sf of max gross building footprint. The proposed square footage is 5,322 
sf with a 5,322 sf footprint. The building will be approximately 27’ at the tallest point. 
 
There is a plat note that states that Lot 9 shall be limited to a single level structure. The 
proposed building on Lot 9 is one-story with a small mezzanine level within the attic trusses 
that will be used for storage. According to the International Building Code (IBC) Section 505.2: 
Mezzanines, the proposed mezzanine should be considered part of the ground-level. The 
proposed mezzanine will be reviewed in more detail during Building Permit review, but Staff 
proposes a Condition of Approval to clarify that the mezzanine shall not provide additional 
retail/business space. The IBC Code is quoted below:   
 
“A mezzanine or mezzanines in compliance with Section 505.2 shall be considered a 
portion of the story below. Such mezzanines shall not contribute to either the building 
area or number of stories as regulated by Section 503.1. The clear height above and 
below the mezzanine floor construction shall be not less than 7 feet (2134 mm).” 
 
In 2003, there was an amendment approved to the Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision 
plat. A condition of approval stated, “The Low Impact Permit application for the various pads 
and buildings will be brought before the Planning Commission for input and recommendation 
to the planning director prior to their construction.” 
 
Staff and service providers reviewed the application and found that, as conditioned, the project 
complies with all applicable standards.  
 
Analysis and Findings 
Low Impact Permit Review 
Section 10-3-4 of the Code contains standards of approval for Low Impact Permits. Staff has not 
separately addressed these standards because if the requirements in the Agreement are 
satisfied (refer to the Consent Agreement Compliance section below), then the Low Impact 
Permit standards are also being satisfied. 
 
Consent Agreement Compliance 
193
3 
 
The Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision is governed by the Gorgoza Pines Consent 
Agreement. The following development standards are identified in the Consent Agreement and 
are applicable to Lot 9. 
 
Densities and Uses: The uses and densities… shall be in accordance with the Consent 
Agreement. Parcels A and B as shown in the Consent Agreement are intended for a variety of 
retail commercial, service commercial, office uses or single or multiple family dwelling units. 
 
Analysis: Tenants have not been decided for the proposed building, and will function as 
a shell structure. Individual business licenses for future uses will be issued pursuant to 
the allowed uses identified in the Consent Agreement. The square footage limitations 
identified on the subdivision plat are being met – the proposed building footprint is 
5,322 square feet. COMPLIES AS CONDITIONED 
 
Setbacks: To the extent that standard setbacks cannot be met within a development, by virtue 
of such approved densities and uses, such setback requirements shall be waived or relaxed as 
long as the County reasonably determines that public safety, health, and welfare concerns can 
be adequately satisfied. 
 
Analysis: The required setbacks in the RR zone are fifty-five (55) feet from the centerline 
of the road (where property line extends to the road) and twelve (12) feet from the side 
and rear property lines. The proposed approximate setbacks are: 
Front (SW) Side (NW) Side (SE) Rear (NE) 
41’ 18’ 38’ 68’ 
 
The proposed setbacks place the structure within the established parking areas. Service 
providers, including the Park City Fire Department and Summit County Engineering 
Department, have reviewed the proposal did not raise public health, safety, or welfare 
concerns. COMPLIES AS CONDITIONED 
 
Landscaping: Landscaping and maintenance requirements shall be in accordance with Summit 
County ordinances. 
 
Analysis: The applicant is proposing landscaping that includes trees, shrubs, and 
perennials. There is existing turf onsite that the applicant proposes to maintain. 
COMPLIES 
 
Parking: Consideration should be given to limiting such parking surface area to the extent 
reasonably possible. Solutions such as on street parking and joint use parking facilities shall be 
considered. 
 
Analysis: The parking for the Quarry Junction project was completed prior to 
development of individual lots. Summit County has a maximum parking requirement 
rather than a minimum for commercial space. There are thirty (30) existing parking stalls 
194
4 
 
contained on the property, three (3) of which are ADA. Quarry Village has historically 
used shared parking. Staff has not recorded any complaints regarding parking conflicts. 
The parking being provided is considered adequate. COMPLIES 
 
Plazas: A number of smaller plazas… are encouraged fo r each specific development as it occurs. 
This could include play areas, sitting areas, or simply pleasant areas to congregate. 
 
Analysis: There are no plazas, play areas, or sitting areas proposed on Lot 9. There are 
sufficient public areas within the development.  COMPLIES 
 
Scale: Commercial development within the Project shall be of a scale that is typical of a 
traditional town. There may be locations for anchor tenants, together with significant space for 
smaller scale users. 
 
Analysis: The anchor tenant in the Quarry Junction project is the existing grocery store. 
The applicant has stated that the intent of the proposed building is to provide spaces to 
accommodate retail and office uses, which would be considered “smaller scale users”.  
The proposed scale of the building compliments existing structures within the 
subdivision. The building includes façade shifts and height changes.  COMPLIES AS 
CONDITIONED 
 
Building Heights: Generally building heights will be restricted to two (2) stories. A third story is 
allowed so long as it occurs within the area formed by a pitched roof on the two (2) story 
structure where approved by Summit County. The Gorgoza Pines Consent Agreement allows 
heights to be up to 35 feet above natural or finished grade, whichever is lower. 
 
Analysis: A plat note on the Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision states that 
structures on Lot 9 are limited to one story. There is not a maximum height assigned for 
one-story structures on the subdivision plat, nor in the Gorgoza Pines Agreement. The 
proposed height of the building is approximately 27’ at the highest point. COMPLIES 
 
Signage and Lighting: Outdoor signs and lighting shall be in accordance with Summit County 
ordinances. 
 
Analysis: There is an existing elevated parking lot light onsite. Four types of building 
mounted lights are proposed. The proposed lighting is fully shielded and directs the light 
downward. The Snyderville Basin Development Code states that the maximum outdoor 
light output for commercial uses cannot exceed 100,000 lumens per acre. Lot 9 is 0.57 
acres, so the maximum output may be 57,000 lumens. The total lighting output, 
including the parking lot light, is 25,625 lumens. Staff has included conditions of 
approval to limit the temperature and brightness of each exterior fixture. Signs are 
processed through a building permit application and will be reviewed for compliance 
with applicable standards at the time a complete building permit application is received.  
COMPLIES AS CONDITIONED 
195
5 
 
 
Architecture: Buildings should be designed to invite public comprehension and reflect their 
function or use. Access, when necessary, should be easily discernible and obvious without the 
necessity of excessive signage. Facades should be scaled to and designed to accommodate use 
by humans. Vehicular and equipment structure design should mask the scale of their use. 
Decorative elements may be used to enhance, but should not suggest cosmetic treatment. 
 
Analysis: The proposed building will include various shades of gray and neutral finishes 
including metal roofing, plank and lap siding, and bricks. Windows, doors, and awnings 
will have black steel finishes. There is existing power and communication boxes on-site. 
A 9’ x 9’ transformer pad is proposed near the existing utility infrastructure to 
accommodate a transformer box that Rocky Mountain Power wants to put on-site 
within the ROW for Santa Fe Rd, which is managed by the Quarry Village HOA. The HOA 
has confirmed that the location of the transformer box is acceptable within their ROW.  
The pad will be semi-shielded by proposed trees as seen in the landscaping plan 
included in Exhibit B. The applicant has confirmed that there will not be any façade 
mechanical equipment proposed on the building. Staff have also included conditions of 
approval that prohibits façade mechanical equipment and that if any is proposed that a 
Low Impact Permit Amendment would be required to ensure proper screening. 
COMPLIES AS CONDITIONED

All developments shall comply with the standards found in Chapter 4 of the Code. However, the 
Agreement separately addresses certain standards, as identified above. Below are only those 
items that are not addressed in the Agreement.

adequate loading and unloading 
space is available within the 
development. 
The applicant has not delineated 
any loading and unloading areas. 
Staff has included conditions of 
approval which prohibit large-truck 
deliveries. Any proposed large truck 
deliveries will require an 
amendment to the permit and 
associated plans to accommodate 
for a loading and unloading area. 
The applicant has also confirmed 
that they will include the 
prohibition of large-truck deliveries 
into their lease agreements as well. 
COMPLIES 
Parking Lot Snow Storage 
Uncovered parking lots shall 
provide snow storage equal to 
10% of the uncovered parking lot 
surface. 
Snow storage areas for the 
subdivision have been previously 
identified. This proposal is not in 
conflict with those storage areas.  
The parking lots in the development 
are intended to be shared among 
the various tenants and snow 
removal occurs throughout 
the development. The roads and 
parking lots are private and will be 
maintained by the existing 
Homeowner’s Association, which 
includes the removal of snow.

public utility companies that will

development.

proposed plans and did not raise 
any concerns. Rocky Mountain 
Power will be placing a transformer 
box on-site within the ROW for 
Santa Fe Rd, which is managed by 
the Quarry Village HOA. The HOA 
has confirmed that the location of 
the transformer box is acceptable 
within their ROW.

Mail Delivery 
Locations for mail delivery should 
be shown on the final site plan 
and written approval from the 
postmaster.  
The applicant received confirmation 
from the postmaster to add 
mailboxes to an existing, shared 
mailbox within the Quarry Junction 
area. No final site plan is required.  
COMPLIES 
Solid Waste and Recycling 
Garbage and Recycling Containers 
need to be located within a 
completely enclosed structure.  
There are existing garbage facilities 
located within 150’ of the proposed 
building.  
COMPLIES 
Police and Security 
 
Police have existing, sufficient 
access to the Quarry Junction area.  
COMPLIES

All development shall provide 
neighborhood park facilities in a 
manner that is sufficient to meet 
the specific recreational demands 
generated by the development. 
No new recreational demand is 
being generated by this proposal. 
Parks, trails, and open space were 
planned in the Gorgoza Pines 
Consent Agreement.   
COMPLIES

All building intended for public use 
shall be accessible to the disabled, 
as per the requirements of the 
international building code and the 
Americans with disabilities act. 
There is adequate ADA parking 
within the subdivision. Because the 
building is only one story, there is 
adequate ground floor access. 
Thorough ADA review will take 
place during Building Permit review.

The Snyderville Basin General Plan future land use map identifies Quarry Village as Mixed-Use

Public Notice, Meetings and Comments  
This application was publicly noticed as a public hearing with possible recommendation by the 
SBPC. Notice was physically posted and published online prior to the meeting. Courtesy 
postcards were mailed to property owners within 1000’ of the subject property. The item was 
published in the Park Record.  
 
As of the date of this report, no public comment was received by Staff.  
 
Recommendation 
Staff recommends that the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission review the proposed Low 
Impact Permit, conduct a public hearing, and make a positive recommendation to the 
Community Development Director to approve the application pursuant to the following 
Findings of Fact, Conclusions of Law, and Conditions of Approval. 
 
Findings of Fact: 
1. On July 22, 2025, the Summit County Community Development Department received a 
complete application for a Low Impact Permit on Parcel QJPB-A-9-1AM.  
2. Parcel QJPB-A-9-1AM is considered Lot 9 of the Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Plat A 
Subdivision, which was recorded on May 21, 2004. 
3. The Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Plat A Subdivision is part of the Gorgoza Pines 
Consent Agreement, which was adopted via Ordinance 306 on February 24, 1997.  
4. Parcel QJPB-A-9-1AM (the property) is 0.57 acres. The property is owned by RJ Ford 
Properties LLC. John Ford signed the application form.  
5. In 2003, there was an amendment approved to the Quarry Junction at Pinebrook 
Subdivision plat. A condition of approval stated, “The Low Impact Permit application for 
the various pads and buildings will be brought before the Planning Commission for input 
and recommendation to the planning director prior to their construction.” 
6. The intent of this application is to permit a commercial shell building on the property.  
7. The Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision designates maximum gross square 
footages and maximum gross building footprints for each lot. Lot 9 is allowed 5,462 
square feet of max gross building area and 5,462 square feet of max gross building 
footprint. There is a plat note that states that Lot 9 shall be limited to a single level 
structure. 
8. The proposed square footage is 5,322 sf. The proposed footprint is 5,322 sf.  
199
9 
 
9. The proposed building on Lot 9 is one-story and will have two tenant spaces. 
10. Individual business licenses for future uses will be issued pursuant to the allowed uses 
identified in the Consent Agreement. 
11. The proposed setbacks for the building are as follows: Front (southwest) = 24’, 
Northwest side = 18’, Southeast side = 38’, Rear = 68’.  
12. The proposed height of the building is 27’. 
13. The proposed scale of the building compliments existing structures within the 
subdivision. The building includes façade shifts and height changes. 
14. The Snyderville Basin Development Code states that the maximum outdoor light output 
for commercial uses cannot exceed 100,000 lumens per acre. Lot 9 is 0.57 acres, so the 
maximum output may be 57,000 lumens. The total lighting output, including the parking 
lot light, is 25,625 lumens. 
15. Signs are processed through a building permit application and will be reviewed for 
compliance with applicable standards at the time a complete building permit application 
is received.   
16. The parking for the Quarry Junction project was completed prior to development of 
individual lots. There are thirty (30) existing parking stalls contained on the property, 
three (3) of which are ADA. Quarry Village has historically used shared parking.  
17. There are no plazas, play areas, or sitting areas proposed on Lot 9. There are sufficient 
public areas within the development.   
18. The anchor tenant in the Quarry Junction project is the existing grocery store. 
19. The proposed building will include various shades of gray and neutral finishes including 
metal roofing, plank and lap siding, and bricks. Windows, doors, and awnings will have 
black steel finishes.  
20. A 9’ x 9’ transformer pad is proposed near the existing utility infrastructure and is semi-
screened by trees and landscaping. The pad will be semi-shielded by proposed trees as 
seen in the landscaping plan. There will not be any façade mechanical equipment.  
21. The proposal will not encroach into any wetlands, slopes in excess of 30%, ridgelines, 
avalanche zones, or geologic hazard areas. 
22. The Gorgoza Water Company has confirmed service or the property. 
23. The Snyderville Basin Water Reclamation District has confirmed that they will be able to 
serve the project and have no concerns. 
24. There will not be any large truck deliveries that affect loading or unloading.  
25. The parking lots in the development are intended to be shared among the various 
tenants and snow removal occurs throughout the development. The roads and parking 
lots are private and will be maintained by the existing Homeowner’s Association, which 
includes the removal of snow.       
26. The existing ingress/egress point for the property is off of Santa Fe Rd. 
27. There are existing mailboxes and solid waste/recycling facilities for the proposed 
building.  
28. The proposed building will not affect site triangles or visibility along Santa Fe Rd and W 
Pinebrook Blvd.  
29. Staff and service providers reviewed the proposal.  
 
200
10 
 
Conclusions of Law:  
1. The use is in accordance with the general plan. 
2. The use conforms to all applicable provisions of the Code, including, but not limited to, 
any applicable provisions of Section 10-3-5, Chapter 4, and state and federal regulations. 
3. The use is not detrimental to public health, safety, and welfare. 
4. The use is appropriately located with respect to public facilities. 
5. The use is compatible with the existing neighborhood character and with the purpose 
provisions of the applicable zone district, and will not affect surrounding land uses. 
 
Conditions of Approval: 
1. The proposed mezzanine shall not provide any additional retail or business space. The 
mezzanine shall be used for storage or similar uses that are inaccessible to customers of 
the business.  
2. The locations, heights, and design of the proposed project shall be shown in Exhibit A. 
3. No outdoor storage is permitted. 
4. Due to lack of delivery space, large-truck deliveries that will block circulation shall be 
prohibited. 
5. Any utilization of large-truck deliveries will require an amendment to this permit to 
accommodate a redesign and incorporation of large-truck deliveries. 
6. Signage and signage relocation approvals are not included as part of this approval and 
shall be reviewed through separate sign permits. 
7. Exterior lighting temperature shall not exceed three thousand degrees (3,000°) Kelvin, 
except the parking lot light pole being replaced which may not exceed five thousand 
degrees (5,000°) Kelvin. 
8. All disturbed areas shall be revegetated with a native, drought-tolerant seed mix or as 
per the submitted Landscape Plan included in Exhibit B.  
9. All necessary permits must be obtained, and fees shall be paid prior to the 
commencement of any construction activity, including but not limited to the Summit 
Building Department and Summit County Engineering Department. 
10. All other service provider requirements shall be met. 
11. Business licenses for future uses shall be issued pursuant to the allowed uses identified 
in the Gorgoza Pines Ranch Consent Agreement. 
12. Any business licenses issued that are not according to the uses identified in the Gorgoza 
Pines Ranch Consent Agreement shall be revoked. 
13. Prior to final approval of a building permit, the applicant shall submit construction plans 
to the Engineering Department in conjunction with a Development Improvement 
Agreement. 
14. All mechanical equipment, including equipment related with future tenant 
improvements, shall be screened. 
15. Façade mechanical equipment, including vents, shall be prohibited unless otherwise 
approved through a Low Impact Permit Amendment. 
16. No modification to the approved Low Impact Permit is permitted without the prior 
approval of the Summit County Community Development Department.

Welcome/Attendance
Regular Session (6:00 P.M.)
1) Public comment for items not on the agenda or pending
applications. (6:00 P.M.)
Commission Chair Mooney opened the meeting for public comment at
6:00 p.m.
No public comments were heard.
Commission Chair Mooney closed the meeting for public comment at
6:01 p.m.
2) Public hearing and action regarding a Conditional Use Permit
(CUP) for an "Accessory Dwelling Unit" (ADU) within Unit 6 of the
Altitude Loft Condominiums. 4123 N Forestdale Drive, Unit 6.
211
Parcel ALTLC-6. Applicant: Brock Milton. Owner: Mark A. Abbott.
Administrative Process. Project #25-095.Laura Kuhrmeyer, County
Planner. (6:01 P.M.)
Attachment: Cover Page
Attachment: Project 25-095 CUP Altitude Lofts Unit 6 ADU Staff Report
and Exhibits 8.12.25
Laura Kuhrmeyer, Planner, presented an application for a Conditional
Use Permit for an Accessory Dwelling Unit in the Altitude Loft
Condominiums. (6:02 P.M.)
Commissioners Sagerman, Peteroy, and Young asked brief clarifying
questions. Planner Kuhrmeyer addressed their questions. (6:04 P.M.)
Commission Chair Mooney opened the meeting for public hearing at
6:08 p.m.
Brock Milton, Applicant, introduced himself to the Commission and
provided additional information on the project. (6:08 P.M.)
Commission Chair Mooney closed the meeting for public hearing at 6:09
p.m.
Commission Chair Mooney provided positive feedback to Planner
Kuhrmeyer regarding the conditions of approval provided in the packet.
(6:09 P.M.)
Spencer Young made a motion to approve the applicant's
Conditional Use Permit for an Accessory Dwelling Unit in the
Altitude Lofts Unit 6 according to the findings of fact, conclusions
of law, and conditions of approval in the staff report. Eric
Sagerman seconded, and all voted in favor, (6-0).
3) **Applicant requested continuation to the September 9th meeting**.
Public hearing and possible action regarding a Conditional Use
Permit (CUP) for an expansion of an existing Trailhead Parking Lot
for the East Canyon Creek Trailhead. Approximate address: 8566 N
Bluebird Lane. Parcel PP-50-D-X and Parcel SS-1-A-7-X. Applicant:
Phares Gines, representing Snyderville Basin Special Recreation
District. Owner: Snyderville Basin Special Recreation District and
Snyderville Basin Water Reclamation District (SBWRD).
Administrative Process. Project #23-198.Laura Kuhrmeyer, County
Planner.
Attachment: Cover Page
This agenda item was continued by the applicant.
4) **This Public Hearing has been moved to the August 26, 2025**.
Public hearing and possible action regarding a Conditional Use
Permit for a 5,710 square foot Major Accessory Building; 4865 N
500 W, Park City; Parcel PP-87-8. Applicant: Rosemary Stum, Lloyd
212
Architecture. Owner: Nathan Wright. Administrative Process.
Project #25-067. Jennifer Leslie, County Planner.
Attachment: Cover Page
Attachment: Wright Barn CUP Staff Report
This agenda item was continued due to noticing needs.
5) Approval of Minutes: July 8, 2025. (6:11 P.M.)
Attachment: Cover Page
Attachment: Draft SBPC Minutes 07.08.25.pdf
Spencer Young made a motion to approve the minutes from July 8,
2025. Makena Hawley seconded, and all voted in favor, (4-0).
Commission Items (6:12 P.M.)
Commission Chair Mooney asked the Commissioners for input and ideas for
future training work sessions. (6:12 P.M.)
Director Items (6:13 P.M.)
Ray Milliner, Principal Planner, stated that the Commission would start seeing
applications from Dakota Pacific since the County Manager had approved their
application, and the appeals window had expired. Planner Milliner also
provided a preview of agenda items for upcoming meetings. (6:13 P.M.)
Lynda Viti, Civil Deputy Attorney, informed the Commission of changes to the
privacy act regarding the collecting of personal information from members of
the public during meetings. (6:23 P.M.)
Adjournment (6:29 P.M.)
Heather Peteroy made a motion to adjourn. Eric Sagerman seconded, and
all voted in favor, (6-0).
